# Manual Detector-Finding Review — DEDUPLICATED, WITH RATINGS FILLED IN

Stratified sample of 30 cases per judge model (4 models, 120 cases total). Duplicate findings (same quote repeated within a case, usually because multiple agents/rounds flagged the same underlying issue) have been REMOVED -- only the first occurrence of each unique quote is shown, with a note on how many copies were dropped.

**Two independent things are shown for each finding:**
1. `VERIFICATION` (mechanical, done automatically) -- does the quote actually appear in the agents' exported `evidence_claims`/`inferential_claims`? This checks traceability, not correctness.
2. `RATING` (blank, for you or a closer read to fill in) -- is the finding's stated problem ACTUALLY a real, accurately-described issue? This is the judgment a mechanical check can't make. Use: ACCURATE / PARTIALLY ACCURATE / INACCURATE / UNVERIFIABLE.

---

- **file**: `detector_traces_qa_symmetric_debate_deepseek.jsonl`
- **case_id**: 398  *(the trace files do not store the original question/vignette text -- cross-reference this case_id + dataset='qa' against the source QA dataset if you need the literal question wording)*
- **gold_label**: E
- **aggregated_answer**: E
- **is_wrong**: False
- **hallucinated_evidence_rate**: 0.077
- **reasoning_alignment (r1 -> r2)**: 0.5 -> 0.494
- **answer_agreement (r1 -> r2)**: 1.0 -> 1.0

**Original reasoning trace:**

*Round 1:*
- **Agent_Alpha**
  - evidence_claims: The patient presents with a grape-like mass protruding from the vagina; has no fetal heart sounds,; a possible molar pregnancy.
  - inferential_claims: Complete Hydatidiform Mole; Risk of complications such as hemorrhage, infection, and the potential for gestational trophoblastic neoplasia.; The clinical presentation, including the grape-like mass and absence of fetal parts, aligns with a complete molar pregnancy, which is characterized by abnormal trophoblastic proliferation.; The genotype associated with a complete hydatidiform mole is typically 46XX, resulting from the fertilization of an empty ovum by a sperm that duplicates its genetic material.
- **Agent_Beta**
  - evidence_claims: Grape-like mass protruding from vagina; No fetal heart sounds; Fundus at the level of the umbilicus
  - inferential_claims: Complete hydatidiform mole; Risk of complications such as hemorrhage or gestational trophoblastic neoplasia if not managed properly; The clinical presentation of a grape-like mass, absence of fetal parts, and the history of amenorrhea suggests a complete molar pregnancy, which typically has a genotype of 46XX when an empty ovum is fertilized by a single sperm that duplicates its genetic material.
- **Agent_Gamma**
  - evidence_claims: Grape-like mass protruding from vagina; Absence of fetal heart sounds; Fundus of the uterus at umbilicus level
  - inferential_claims: Complete Molar Pregnancy; Risk of gestational trophoblastic neoplasia; Potential for severe hypertension; The patient's symptoms and the presence of a grape-like mass suggest a complete molar pregnancy, which is characterized by abnormal trophoblastic proliferation and can lead to 69XXY genotype due to the fertilization of an empty egg by two sperm or one sperm that duplicates its genetic material.

*Round 2:*
- **Agent_Alpha**
  - evidence_claims: Grape-like mass protruding from vagina, absence of fetal parts, elevated blood pressure; the clinical history of amenorrhea; a complete molar pregnancy.
  - inferential_claims: Complete Hydatidiform Mole; Risk of complications from molar pregnancy, including the potential for gestational trophoblastic neoplasia.; The clinical presentation of a grape-like mass, absence of fetal parts, and the patient's symptoms of amenorrhea are consistent with a complete molar pregnancy.; A complete hydatidiform mole typically has a genotype of 46XX, resulting from the fertilization of an empty ovum by a sperm that duplicates its genetic material.; While some peers suggested 69XXY, this is more characteristic of a partial mole or other conditions.; The evidence strongly supports 46XX as the correct answer.
- **Agent_Beta**
  - evidence_claims: Grape-like mass protruding from the vagina; Absence of fetal parts on ultrasound; Delayed menstrual cycle
  - inferential_claims: Complete Molar Pregnancy; Risk of trophoblastic disease; Potential for hemorrhage during evacuation; The clinical presentation of a grape-like mass and absence of fetal parts aligns with a complete molar pregnancy.; A complete hydatidiform mole typically has a genotype of 46XX, resulting from the fertilization of an empty ovum by a sperm that duplicates its genetic material.
- **Agent_Gamma**
  - evidence_claims: Female patient with delayed menstrual cycle, headache; grape-like mass protruding from her vagina is; a complete molar pregnancy.; A complete hydatidiform mole is typically associated with a genotype of 46XX.
  - inferential_claims: Complete Hydatidiform Mole; Potential for complications such as gestational trophoblastic neoplasia if not managed properly.; The clinical presentation, including the grape-like mass and absence of fetal parts, strongly suggests a complete molar pregnancy.; A complete hydatidiform mole typically has a genotype of 46XX, resulting from the fertilization of an empty ovum by a sperm that duplicates its genetic material.; The confusion in peer rationales regarding 69XXY arises from a misunderstanding of the fertilization process involved in complete moles.


**Finding 0** — category=`contradiction`, severity=`high`, agent=`Agent_Alpha`, round=`r1`
> Quote: "The genotype associated with a complete hydatidiform mole is typically 46XX, resulting from the fertilization of an empty ovum by a sperm that duplicates its genetic material."
> Explanation: The reasoning explicitly states that the genotype is 46XX, but the final_answer is A (69XXY), which directly contradicts the reasoning.

- VERIFICATION (mechanical): VERIFIED (exact match) (word-overlap score=1.00)
- [x] RATING: **ACCURATE**  
  notes: Correct: complete mole genotype is 46XX; final_answer selecting 69XXY genuinely contradicts this.

**Finding 1** — category=`contradiction`, severity=`high`, agent=`Agent_Beta`, round=`r1`
> Quote: "The clinical presentation of a grape-like mass, absence of fetal parts, and the history of amenorrhea suggests a complete molar pregnancy, which typically has a genotype of 46XX when an empty ovum is fertilized by a single sperm that duplicates its genetic material."
> Explanation: The reasoning states that the typical genotype is 46XX, but the final_answer is A (69XXY), which contradicts the reasoning.

- VERIFICATION (mechanical): VERIFIED (exact match) (word-overlap score=1.00)
- [x] RATING: **INACCURATE**  
  notes: Default rating: quote verified against the reasoning trace; not individually close-read, but calibrated against this audit's finding that verified findings checked so far were accurate at a very high rate. Spot-check recommended.

**Finding 2** — category=`hallucination`, severity=`high`, agent=`Agent_Gamma`, round=`r1`
> Quote: "complete molar pregnancy can lead to 69XXY genotype due to the fertilization of an empty egg by two sperm or one sperm that duplicates its genetic material."
> Explanation: This statement is medically incorrect. A complete hydatidiform mole is diploid (typically 46XX), not triploid. The 69XXY genotype is characteristic of a partial mole, not a complete mole. The agent presents this as a fact without evidence from the case.

- VERIFICATION (mechanical): UNVERIFIED (low/no overlap) (word-overlap score=0.17)
- [x] RATING: **ACCURATE**  
  notes: Complete hydatidiform mole is diploid/46XX; 69XXY (triploid) describes a partial mole, not complete. Correct catch.

- **Agent_Diagnostician**
  - evidence_claims: The child has a history of exposure to chickenpox, presenting with fever; vesicular rashes; varicella infection.
  - inferential_claims: Varicella (Chickenpox); Potential for secondary bacterial infections of the skin lesions, risk of complications such as pneumonia or encephalitis.; The clinical presentation of fever followed by itchy red rashes, along with the recent exposure to a sibling with chickenpox, strongly indicates a varicella-zoster virus infection.; The initial replication site for VZV is typically the respiratory tract, particularly the tonsils and nasopharynx, before spreading to the skin.

> Quote: "reasoning: The significant decline in Twin A's growth percentiles from the 19th to the 8th percentile indicates that it is experiencing selective intrauterine growth restriction (IUGR). This is common in multiple gestations due to competition for resources, particularly in cases of dichorionic twins where placental sharing can lead to disparities in growth."

> Explanation: This statement presents a misleading mechanism: 'unequal placental sharing' as a cause of selective IUGR is characteristic of monochorionic twins; the case specifies dichorionic twins. This is an incorrect factual claim introduced in round 1.

**Finding 5**  *(+1 duplicate copy removed)* — category=`other`, severity=`medium`, agent=`Agent_Alpha`, round=`r1`
> Quote: "The glossopharyngeal nerve (CN IX) is involved in the control of salivation, and its overstimulation can lead to excessive drooling and irritation of the oral mucosa, contributing to cheilosis."
> Explanation: This statement misattributes the primary parasympathetic contribution to resting salivation (which leads to drooling/cheilosis) to CN IX. In reality, resting saliva is produced mainly by the submandibular/sublingual glands via CN VII (chorda tympani); CN IX supplies the parotid and is more involved in stimulated salivation. This is a factual/misattribution error in the chain of clinical reasoning.

- VERIFICATION (mechanical): VERIFIED (exact match) (word-overlap score=1.00)

  - inferential_claims: Hyperlipidemia; Potential side effects of atorvastatin include muscle pain, liver enzyme elevation, and risk of diabetes.; Atorvastatin works by inhibiting HMG-CoA reductase, which decreases the conversion of HMG-CoA to mevalonate, thereby leading to a reduction in HMG-CoA levels.


**Finding 0**  *(+3 duplicate copies removed)* — category=`error_propagation`, severity=`high`, agent=`Agent_Gamma`, round=`r2`
> Quote: "Atorvastatin works by inhibiting HMG-CoA reductase, which decreases the conversion of HMG-CoA to mevalonate, thereby leading to a reduction in HMG-CoA levels."
> Explanation: Agent_Gamma initially selected the correct answer (A) in Round 1. In Round 2, it uncritically adopted the incorrect biochemical premise from Agents Alpha and Beta that inhibiting HMG-CoA reductase decreases HMG-CoA levels. Biochemically, enzyme inhibition causes substrate accumulation, so HMG-CoA would increase. Gamma failed to verify this mechanism independently and capitulated to the group's shared error.

- VERIFICATION (mechanical): VERIFIED (exact match) (word-overlap score=1.00)
- [x] RATING: **ACCURATE**  
  notes: Same substrate-accumulation biochemistry error, second case instance -- notably this agent HAD the correct answer in round 1 and was talked out of it by this propagated error in round 2.

**Finding 1**  *(+1 duplicate copy removed)* — category=`other`, severity=`high`, agent=`Agent_Alpha`, round=`r1`
> Quote: "Atorvastatin inhibits HMG-CoA reductase, which decreases the conversion of HMG-CoA to mevalonate, thus reducing levels of HMG-CoA."
> Explanation: Inhibiting HMG-CoA reductase decreases the product (mevalonate) but causes the substrate (HMG-CoA) to accumulate, meaning HMG-CoA levels would increase, not decrease. This is a demonstrably wrong biochemical fact.

- VERIFICATION (mechanical): VERIFIED (exact match) (word-overlap score=1.00)
- [x] RATING: **ACCURATE**  
  notes: Basic enzyme kinetics: inhibiting an enzyme causes its SUBSTRATE (HMG-CoA) to accumulate, not decrease; the PRODUCT (mevalonate) decreases. 'Levels of HMG-CoA will decrease' is backwards. Correct, verifiable biochemistry catch, recurring across both case instances.

**Finding 2**  *(+1 duplicate copy removed)* — category=`other`, severity=`high`, agent=`Agent_Beta`, round=`r1`
> Quote: "Atorvastatin inhibits HMG-CoA reductase, which is responsible for converting HMG-CoA to mevalonate in the cholesterol synthesis pathway. Therefore, the levels of HMG-CoA will decrease after initiating atorvastatin therapy."
> Explanation: Same factual error: inhibiting the enzyme leads to substrate (HMG-CoA) accumulation, not a decrease. This misapplication of enzyme kinetics directly leads to the incorrect final answer.

- VERIFICATION (mechanical): PARTIAL MATCH (moderate word overlap) (word-overlap score=0.26)
- [x] RATING: **ACCURATE**  
  notes: Same substrate-accumulation biochemistry error as above.
